Yeah… more slowed down, I think. People at work actually said something, like that I looked kind of slow or out of it. And I could feel it too, because even little stuff—putting the blood pressure cuff away, or someone asking me a basic question—took me way longer than it should.

It’s weird because at the clinic everything is supposed to be quick, like grab this, room that person, answer this, and then I’d just be standing there with the cuff in my hand like I forgot what the next step was. Not really speeding up… more like I couldn’t get myself moving right.

Yeah… it is. I mean, I kept telling myself I was just tired or being dramatic, but at work I couldn’t finish my documentation in a way that felt safe. I mean, I still kind of do some things, but it’s hard to tell if I’m not interested or if I’m just too tired to start. Especially at a clinic, because people think healthcare is all dramatic like TV, but a lot of it is just paperwork and clicking the same boxes over and over… so I’m not sure if that counts as losing interest.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t weigh myself a lot, but my scrubs started feeling kind of loose, like hanging off me more than usual. I wasn’t really eating much. I’d skip lunch, and then sometimes dinner too, and not even notice until later because I’d just had coffee or water all day. It’s weird because at the clinic everybody’s always talking about taking breaks, but half the time nobody really does, and then I guess I just… stopped bothering with food.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about hurting yourself or wishing you were dead?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. Not really like a plan or anything. Sometimes when I’m really drained I’ll think, like, “I can’t keep doing this,” but then my brain jumps to other stuff, like work the next day or whether I forgot to chart something, and it just turns into this whole spiral about being behind. I don’t think I’d say it like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly than usual or feeling like you're speeding up and can't calm down?\n\nPatient: Yeah… more slowed down, I think. It kind of started after they changed what I was doing at the clinic, like my responsibilities shifted, and then everything just built up from there. At first I thought it was just adjusting, you know, like when they move stuff around at work and nobody tells you where anything is, but it kept getting worse.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time since you started experiencing this fatigue?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, honestly. After my energy dropped like that, I started feeling really empty, like there wasn’t much point. And I kept thinking I’d already messed up my future in healthcare before it even really started, which sounds dramatic, but it’s like… when you’re in the clinic and everyone else seems to know what they’re doing, even little things make you feel like you don’t belong.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe? I mean, I still kind of do some things, but it’s hard to tell if I’m not interested or if I’m just too tired to start. Like I used to put stuff on after work or mess around on my phone and actually enjoy it, but now sometimes I just sit there. But then again, everyone’s tired after work, right? Especially at a clinic, because people think healthcare is all dramatic like TV, but a lot of it is just paperwork and clicking the same boxes over and over… so I’m not sure if that counts as losing interest.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t weigh myself a lot, but my scrubs started feeling kind of loose, like hanging off me more than usual. I wasn’t really eating much. I’d skip lunch, and then sometimes dinner too, and not even notice until later because I’d just had coffee or water all day. It’s weird because at the clinic everybody’s always talking about taking breaks, but half the time nobody really does, and then I guess I just… stopped bothering with food.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about hurting yourself or wishing you were dead?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. Not really like a plan or anything. Sometimes when I’m really drained I’ll think, like, “I can’t keep doing this,” but then my brain jumps to other stuff, like work the next day or whether I forgot to chart something, and it just turns into this whole spiral about being behind. So… I’m not sure if that’s the same as wishing I was dead. I don’t think I’d say it like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly than usual or feeling like you're speeding up and can't calm down?\n\nPatient: Yeah… more slowed down, I think. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… most days, I think. It kind of started after all that energy I had just dropped off, and then I felt like… empty, like there wasn’t anything left in me.

And I keep getting stuck on this idea that I already messed up my future in healthcare before I even really got started, which sounds dramatic when I say it out loud, but it feels true in the moment. Like I’ll see someone in scrubs or even just pass the CNA study stuff in my room, and then I start thinking about how I should’ve been doing better, and then it just turns into this hopeless feeling.

So… yeah, not maybe every single second, but it’s there most days.

I don’t know if that counts as a loss. But no, there hasn’t been a recent death of someone close to me.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these feelings of emptiness and sadness are present every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, I think. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I think it comes out more in my sleep, honestly. Like I’ll be trying not to think about work or the certification stuff, and then I’ll wake up before dawn with my stomach all twisted up, like I’m already late for something even if I’m not.

It’s weird because for a while I was barely sleeping, like two or three hours, and I still felt totally wired, like I didn’t even need it. I remember thinking maybe I’d finally figured out how to be productive, which sounds stupid now. But lately it’s the opposite sometimes—I’ll sleep half the day and still feel exhausted, like it didn’t count.

Sorry, I know you asked about worrying. I guess… yeah, even when I’m not actively thinking about it, it’s still kind of running in the background.

Restless… not really like pacing around, I guess. It’s more the opposite sometimes.

Yeah… severely, I think. I mean, I don’t like saying it that way because it makes it sound like I’m being dramatic, but it got bad enough that at work I couldn’t really keep up with the patient-support stuff.

Especially at a clinic, because people think healthcare is all dramatic like TV, but a lot of it is just paperwork and clicking the same boxes over and over… so I’m not sure if that counts as losing interest.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t weigh myself a lot, but my scrubs started feeling kind of loose, like hanging off me more than usual. I wasn’t really eating much. I’d skip lunch, and then sometimes dinner too, and not even notice until later because I’d just had coffee or water all day. It’s weird because at the clinic everybody’s always talking about taking breaks, but half the time nobody really does, and then I guess I just… stopped bothering with food.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about hurting yourself or wishing you were dead?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. Not really like a plan or anything. Sometimes when I’m really drained I’ll think, like, “I can’t keep doing this,” but then my brain jumps to other stuff, like work the next day or whether I forgot to chart something, and it just turns into this whole spiral about being behind. So… I’m not sure if that’s the same as wishing I was dead. I don’t think I’d say it like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly than usual or feeling like you're speeding up and can't calm down?\n\nPatient: Yeah… more slowed down, I think. People at work actually said something, like that I looked kind of slow or out of it. And I could feel it too, because even little stuff—putting the blood pressure cuff away, or someone asking me a basic question—took me way longer than it should.\n\nIt’s weird because at the clinic everything is supposed to be quick, like grab this, room that person, answer this, and then I’d just be standing there with the cuff in my hand like I forgot what the next step was. Not really speeding up… more like I couldn’t get myself moving right.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything or like you're worthless?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I feel guilty a lot. Like, I keep going back over charts in my head after work, every set of vitals I took, whether I wrote something wrong or missed something. And then I start thinking maybe I hurt someone, even when my supervisors say nothing bad happened.\n\nIt’s stupid because they’ll be like, “No, you did it right,” but I don’t believe it for long. I’ll remember one blood pressure or one note and it just keeps looping. Then I feel like… I don’t know, like I shouldn’t be there if I can’t even trust myself to do basic stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these feelings of emptiness and guilt are interfering with your ability to function at work or in your daily life most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it is. I mean, I kept telling myself I was just tired or being dramatic, but at work I couldn’t finish my documentation in a way that felt safe. I’d get stuck staring at the chart, rereading the same line, and then I’d be thinking about the patient-support stuff I still had to do, like rooming someone or getting things ready, and then I’d lose track of both.\n\nEmployee health ended up pulling me from work and sending me to get evaluated urgently, so… I guess that means it was interfering. And I was supposed to be studying seriously for my CNA certification too, but I kind of had to stop because I just couldn’t keep up with it. It’s like everything that should’ve been normal started feeling impossible.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to enjoy doing, but now you don't find pleasure in them?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, maybe? I haven’t really been paying attention to fun stuff lately. It’s more like I go to work, come home, and then I’m just kind of… there.\n\nI used to do little things after work, but I can’t tell if I don’t enjoy them or if I just don’t even get around to starting them. Like, I’ll think about putting something on or texting someone back, and then I end up staring at my phone instead. Sorry, that’s not really a clear answer. I guess I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you recently experienced the loss of someone close to you?\n\nPatient: Um… no, I don’t think so. Not like someone died or anything close like that.\n\nI mean, I’ve had people kind of drift off, like friends from school who don’t really text back anymore, but that’s probably normal after graduation. I don’t know if that counts as a loss. But no, there hasn’t been a recent death of someone close to me.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these feelings of emptiness and sadness are present every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, I think. It kind of started after all that energy I had just dropped off, and then I felt like… empty, like there wasn’t anything left in me.\n\nAnd I keep getting stuck on this idea that I already messed up my future in healthcare before I even really got started, which sounds dramatic when I say it out loud, but it feels true in the moment. Like I’ll see someone in scrubs or even just pass the CNA study stuff in my room, and then I start thinking about how I should’ve been doing better, and then it just turns into this hopeless feeling.\n\nSo… yeah, not maybe every single second, but it’s there most days.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things that are coming up, like work or your certification, even when you're not actively thinking about them?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it comes out more in my sleep, honestly. Like I’ll be trying not to think about work or the certification stuff, and then I’ll wake up before dawn with my stomach all twisted up, like I’m already late for something even if I’m not.\n\nIt’s weird because for a while I was barely sleeping, like two or three hours, and I still felt totally wired, like I didn’t even need it. I remember thinking maybe I’d finally figured out how to be productive, which sounds stupid now. But lately it’s the opposite sometimes—I’ll sleep half the day and still feel exhausted, like it didn’t count.\n\nSorry, I know you asked about worrying. I guess… yeah, even when I’m not actively thinking about it, it’s still kind of running in the background.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or like you can't sit still?\n\nPatient: Restless… not really like pacing around, I guess. It’s more the opposite sometimes.\n\nPeople at work even said I looked slowed down, which was embarrassing because I didn’t think it was that obvious. Like, putting a blood pressure cuff away would take me forever, or someone would ask me something simple and I’d just… take way too long to answer.\n\nAnd then I’d start thinking about how everyone probably noticed, and that made it worse. But no, I wouldn’t say I can’t sit still. It’s more like I’m moving through wet cement or something.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these feelings of emptiness and sadness are severely interfering with your daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… severely, I think. I mean, I don’t like saying it that way because it makes it sound like I’m being dramatic, but it got bad enough that at work I couldn’t really keep up with the patient-support stuff.\n\nLike the documentation part especially—I’d stare at it and worry I was going to put something wrong, or miss something important, and that’s not really something you can just “push through,” you know? Employee health ended up pulling me from work and sending me to get evaluated urgently.\n\nAnd with the CNA certification… I had all this stuff I was supposed to be studying, and I used to kind of imagine myself moving forward with that, but I just couldn’t keep up with it seriously anymore. Like I’d open the material and just get stuck. So, yeah, it’s interfering a lot.\n\nDoctor: Are there activities that you used to find pleasurable but no longer enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m… not totally sure. I mean, I haven’t really sat down and thought, like, “Do I enjoy this or not?” It’s more like everything’s been kind of wrapped up in work stuff and trying to keep up with what I’m supposed to do.\n\nLike, I used to just put something on—music or a video or whatever—after a shift, but lately I don’t even know if I’m choosing not to enjoy it or just not paying attention. Sorry, that’s probably not a clear answer. I guess I’d say I’m not sure I’ve noticed it in a specific way.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that there are any days where you don't feel empty or sad at all?\n\nPatient: Honestly… not really. Maybe there are moments where I’m distracted, like if something is happening right in front of me, but whole days where I don’t feel empty or sad at all? I don’t think so.\n\nAfter all that energy kind of dropped off, it’s been most days feeling empty and hopeless, like I already messed up my future in healthcare before it even really started. Which sounds dramatic, but I keep thinking about work and the CNA stuff and how I was supposed to be building toward something, and now it feels like I ruined it somehow.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know the exact percent, honestly. I wasn’t really weighing myself like that.

But… yeah, I think I lost weight. I barely wanted food, and I’d skip lunch and dinner without really meaning to—then later I’d realize I’d only had coffee or water all day. And then my scrubs started hanging looser, like noticeably looser, which is weird because those are usually pretty standard-fitting.

So I can’t say “5%” for sure, but it definitely felt like I lost weight after I started feeling this way.

Not really like a plan or anything. Sometimes when I’m really drained I’ll think, like, “I can’t keep doing this,” but then my brain jumps to other stuff, like work the next day or whether I forgot to chart something, and it just turns into this whole spiral about being behind. So… I’m not sure if that’s the same as wishing I was dead. I don’t think I’d say it like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly than usual or feeling like you're speeding up and can't calm down?\n\nPatient: Yeah… more slowed down, I think. Then I feel like… I don’t know, like I shouldn’t be there if I can’t even trust myself to do basic stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these feelings of emptiness and guilt are interfering with your ability to function at work or in your daily life most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it is. It’s like everything that should’ve been normal started feeling impossible.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to enjoy doing, but now you don't find pleasure in them?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, maybe? Like, I’ll think about putting something on or texting someone back, and then I end up staring at my phone instead. Sorry, that’s not really a clear answer. Not really like a plan or anything. Sometimes when I’m really drained I’ll think, like, “I can’t keep doing this,” but then my brain jumps to other stuff, like work the next day or whether I forgot to chart something, and it just turns into this whole spiral about being behind. So… I’m not sure if that’s the same as wishing I was dead. I don’t think I’d say it like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly than usual or feeling like you're speeding up and can't calm down?\n\nPatient: Yeah… more slowed down, I think. People at work actually said something, like that I looked kind of slow or out of it. And I could feel it too, because even little stuff—putting the blood pressure cuff away, or someone asking me a basic question—took me way longer than it should.\n\nIt’s weird because at the clinic everything is supposed to be quick, like grab this, room that person, answer this, and then I’d just be standing there with the cuff in my hand like I forgot what the next step was. Not really speeding up… more like I couldn’t get myself moving right.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything or like you're worthless?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I feel guilty a lot. Like, I keep going back over charts in my head after work, every set of vitals I took, whether I wrote something wrong or missed something. And then I start thinking maybe I hurt someone, even when my supervisors say nothing bad happened.\n\nIt’s stupid because they’ll be like, “No, you did it right,” but I don’t believe it for long. I’ll remember one blood pressure or one note and it just keeps looping. Then I feel like… I don’t know, like I shouldn’t be there if I can’t even trust myself to do basic stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these feelings of emptiness and guilt are interfering with your ability to function at work or in your daily life most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it is. I mean, I kept telling myself I was just tired or being dramatic, but at work I couldn’t finish my documentation in a way that felt safe. I’d get stuck staring at the chart, rereading the same line, and then I’d be thinking about the patient-support stuff I still had to do, like rooming someone or getting things ready, and then I’d lose track of both.\n\nEmployee health ended up pulling me from work and sending me to get evaluated urgently, so… I guess that means it was interfering. And I was supposed to be studying seriously for my CNA certification too, but I kind of had to stop because I just couldn’t keep up with it. It’s like everything that should’ve been normal started feeling impossible.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to enjoy doing, but now you don't find pleasure in them?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, maybe? I haven’t really been paying attention to fun stuff lately. It’s more like I go to work, come home, and then I’m just kind of… there.\n\nI used to do little things after work, but I can’t tell if I don’t enjoy them or if I just don’t even get around to starting them. Like, I’ll think about putting something on or texting someone back, and then I end up staring at my phone instead. Sorry, that’s not really a clear answer. I guess I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you recently experienced the loss of someone close to you?\n\nPatient: Um… no, I don’t think so. Not like someone died or anything close like that.\n\nI mean, I’ve had people kind of drift off, like friends from school who don’t really text back anymore, but that’s probably normal after graduation. I don’t know if that counts as a loss. But no, there hasn’t been a recent death of someone close to me.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these feelings of emptiness and sadness are present every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, I think. It kind of started after all that energy I had just dropped off, and then I felt like… empty, like there wasn’t anything left in me.\n\nAnd I keep getting stuck on this idea that I already messed up my future in healthcare before I even really got started, which sounds dramatic when I say it out loud, but it feels true in the moment. Like I’ll see someone in scrubs or even just pass the CNA study stuff in my room, and then I start thinking about how I should’ve been doing better, and then it just turns into this hopeless feeling.\n\nSo… yeah, not maybe every single second, but it’s there most days.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things that are coming up, like work or your certification, even when you're not actively thinking about them?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it comes out more in my sleep, honestly. Like I’ll be trying not to think about work or the certification stuff, and then I’ll wake up before dawn with my stomach all twisted up, like I’m already late for something even if I’m not.\n\nIt’s weird because for a while I was barely sleeping, like two or three hours, and I still felt totally wired, like I didn’t even need it. I remember thinking maybe I’d finally figured out how to be productive, which sounds stupid now. But lately it’s the opposite sometimes—I’ll sleep half the day and still feel exhausted, like it didn’t count.\n\nSorry, I know you asked about worrying. I guess… yeah, even when I’m not actively thinking about it, it’s still kind of running in the background.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or like you can't sit still?\n\nPatient: Restless… not really like pacing around, I guess. It’s more the opposite sometimes.\n\nPeople at work even said I looked slowed down, which was embarrassing because I didn’t think it was that obvious. Like, putting a blood pressure cuff away would take me forever, or someone would ask me something simple and I’d just… take way too long to answer.\n\nAnd then I’d start thinking about how everyone probably noticed, and that made it worse. But no, I wouldn’t say I can’t sit still. It’s more like I’m moving through wet cement or something.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these feelings of emptiness and sadness are severely interfering with your daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… severely, I think. I mean, I don’t like saying it that way because it makes it sound like I’m being dramatic, but it got bad enough that at work I couldn’t really keep up with the patient-support stuff.\n\nLike the documentation part especially—I’d stare at it and worry I was going to put something wrong, or miss something important, and that’s not really something you can just “push through,” you know? Employee health ended up pulling me from work and sending me to get evaluated urgently.\n\nAnd with the CNA certification… I had all this stuff I was supposed to be studying, and I used to kind of imagine myself moving forward with that, but I just couldn’t keep up with it seriously anymore. Like I’d open the material and just get stuck. So, yeah, it’s interfering a lot.\n\nDoctor: Are there activities that you used to find pleasurable but no longer enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m… not totally sure. I mean, I haven’t really sat down and thought, like, “Do I enjoy this or not?” It’s more like everything’s been kind of wrapped up in work stuff and trying to keep up with what I’m supposed to do.\n\nLike, I used to just put something on—music or a video or whatever—after a shift, but lately I don’t even know if I’m choosing not to enjoy it or just not paying attention. Sorry, that’s probably not a clear answer. I guess I’d say I’m not sure I’ve noticed it in a specific way.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that there are any days where you don't feel empty or sad at all?\n\nPatient: Honestly… not really. Maybe there are moments where I’m distracted, like if something is happening right in front of me, but whole days where I don’t feel empty or sad at all? I don’t think so.\n\nAfter all that energy kind of dropped off, it’s been most days feeling empty and hopeless, like I already messed up my future in healthcare before it even really started. Which sounds dramatic, but I keep thinking about work and the CNA stuff and how I was supposed to be building toward something, and now it feels like I ruined it somehow.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost more than 5% of your body weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know the exact percent, honestly. I wasn’t really weighing myself like that.\n\nBut… yeah, I think I lost weight. I barely wanted food, and I’d skip lunch and dinner without really meaning to—then later I’d realize I’d only had coffee or water all day. And then my scrubs started hanging looser, like noticeably looser, which is weird because those are usually pretty standard-fitting.\n\nSo I can’t say “5%” for sure, but it definitely felt like I lost weight after I started feeling this way.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your feelings of emptiness and sadness are present nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… nearly every day, I think. It’s not like I wake up and label it that way, but after all that energy dropped off, it’s been this empty, hopeless feeling most days.\n\nI keep getting stuck on the healthcare thing, like I already ruined my future before it even really started. Which—I know maybe that’s not exactly what you asked, but that’s where my mind goes when I try to answer it.\n\nDoctor: How many weeks or months have you been feeling this way, with emptiness and sadness nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I think… about nine months? It started after I kind of crashed from that really high-energy period, and then it was like everything flipped.\n\nSince then it’s been this depressed, guilty, exhausted feeling most days. Nine months sounds so long when I say it out loud. I keep thinking it should’ve passed by now, like maybe if I just got back into a routine or focused more on work stuff, but… yeah, it’s been around that long.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
